People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I don’t sit still too well. I’ll be in a chair a few minutes and then I’m up messing with boxes, measuring something out in the garage, loading tools… or I end up going back over to the old place. Just feels like my body’s got a motor in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, I don’t think. I get things on my mind, sure, but I wouldn’t say they repeat over and over where I can’t shake them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're not actively working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I get uneasy, I guess. If I’m just sitting there, it doesn’t last long. I’m up sorting boxes or measuring around the garage, loading tools… sometimes I just head back to the old place. Feels like I’ve got a motor in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things are not done properly unless you do them yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I just feel like if I don’t keep after it, things won’t get handled right. The move, the garage, stuff at church… even the younger guys at work. I’m not trying to be difficult, I just seem to be the one with the drive to make sure it’s done properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if things aren't done the way you want them to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that something bad would happen, exactly. I just… I don’t like leaving things sloppy or half-done. It bothers me, but I can’t say I’m expecting some disaster.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all these tasks to achieve a certain standard or to prevent something from going wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s more about getting things done right. Everybody keeps saying I’m doing too much, but… to me, the move won’t handle itself, the new garage needs to be built right, church things need organizing. And the younger fellows at work still need to be shown how the job ought to be done. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. The move, the garage, church things, even the younger guys at work… it just feels like I’m the one with enough push to get it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're thinking about these tasks over and over, even when you're trying to do something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I suppose I think about what needs doing, but I wouldn’t say it’s over and over like that. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts are ever good enough?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “good enough” is a hard way to put it. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF60.3, F90.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly my family pushed me to come in. They say I’m doing too much. I don’t really see it that way. There’s the move to handle, the new garage needs doing right, things at church need organizing, and the younger guys at work still need someone to show them how the job’s supposed to be done. I’m just trying to keep things moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these tasks yourself, or is it okay with you if someone else does them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d rather handle it myself, yes. Not because folks can’t do anything, but they don’t keep up. The move, the garage, church things, even the younger guys at work… it just feels like I’m the one with enough push to get it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're thinking about these tasks over and over, even when you're trying to do something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I suppose I think about what needs doing, but I wouldn’t say it’s over and over like that. Just things that have to get handled.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for not doing things perfectly?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I like things done right, but I don’t sit around beating myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to perform tasks in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Specific way? Not really, no. I just want things done decent, you know… but I don’t have some set way it has to be every time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble relaxing or unwinding when you're not working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t sit still too well. I’ll be in a chair a few minutes and then I’m up messing with boxes, measuring something out in the garage, loading tools… or I end up going back over to the old place. Just feels like my body’s got a motor in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, I don’t think. I get things on my mind, sure, but I wouldn’t say they repeat over and over where I can’t shake them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're not actively working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I get uneasy, I guess. If I’m just sitting there, it doesn’t last long. I’m up sorting boxes or measuring around the garage, loading tools… sometimes I just head back to the old place. Feels like I’ve got a motor in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things are not done properly unless you do them yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I just feel like if I don’t keep after it, things won’t get handled right. The move, the garage, stuff at church… even the younger guys at work. I’m not trying to be difficult, I just seem to be the one with the drive to make sure it’s done properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if things aren't done the way you want them to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that something bad would happen, exactly. I just… I don’t like leaving things sloppy or half-done. It bothers me, but I can’t say I’m expecting some disaster.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all these tasks to achieve a certain standard or to prevent something from going wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s more about getting things done right. Everybody keeps saying I’m doing too much, but… to me, the move won’t handle itself, the new garage needs to be built right, church things need organizing. And the younger fellows at work still need to be shown how the job ought to be done. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts are ever good enough?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “good enough” is a hard way to put it. I just see what still needs doing, and I keep at it. I guess I don’t spend much time deciding if it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that if you didn't do all these tasks, something would be missing or incomplete?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose it would feel incomplete. The move isn’t going to handle itself, and the garage needs to be done right. Same with things at church, and even the younger guys at work. I just feel like I’m the one with the drive to see it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you're doing all these tasks is more because you want everything to be perfect or because you're trying to avoid mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s either one exactly. I just… see what needs doing, and I do it. I don’t think of it as perfection so much. I just don’t like leaving things half-done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would stop doing all these tasks if someone else could do them just as well as you?\n\nPatient: If someone could really do it the same way… maybe I’d let some of it go. But I don’t see that happening much. The move, the garage, church things… even the younger fellows at work, I just seem to be the one with the drive to keep it moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can take a break and come back to a task later without worrying about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can step away if I have to, but it tends to sit there in my mind. I don’t much like leaving it hanging.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly my family pushed me to come in. They say I’m doing too much. I don’t really see it that way. There’s the move to handle, the new garage needs doing right, things at church need organizing, and the younger guys at work still need someone to show them how the job’s supposed to be done. I’m just trying to keep things moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these tasks yourself, or is it okay with you if someone else does them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d rather handle it myself, yes. Not because folks can’t do anything, but they don’t keep up. The move, the garage, church things, even the younger guys at work… it just feels like I’m the one with enough push to get it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're thinking about these tasks over and over, even when you're trying to do something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I suppose I think about what needs doing, but I wouldn’t say it’s over and over like that. Just things that have to get handled.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for not doing things perfectly?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I like things done right, but I don’t sit around beating myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to perform tasks in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Specific way? Not really, no. I just want things done decent, you know… but I don’t have some set way it has to be every time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble relaxing or unwinding when you're not working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t sit still too well. I’ll be in a chair a few minutes and then I’m up messing with boxes, measuring something out in the garage, loading tools… or I end up going back over to the old place. Just feels like my body’s got a motor in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, I don’t think. I get things on my mind, sure, but I wouldn’t say they repeat over and over where I can’t shake them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're not actively working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I get uneasy, I guess. If I’m just sitting there, it doesn’t last long. I’m up sorting boxes or measuring around the garage, loading tools… sometimes I just head back to the old place. Feels like I’ve got a motor in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things are not done properly unless you do them yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I just feel like if I don’t keep after it, things won’t get handled right. The move, the garage, stuff at church… even the younger guys at work. I’m not trying to be difficult, I just seem to be the one with the drive to make sure it’s done properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if things aren't done the way you want them to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that something bad would happen, exactly. I just… I don’t like leaving things sloppy or half-done. And the younger fellows at work still need to be shown how the job ought to be done. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts are ever good enough?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “good enough” is a hard way to put it. I don’t much like leaving it hanging.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're driven more by wanting to achieve something perfectly or by wanting to avoid making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about perfect, exactly, or being afraid of mistakes. I’m just trying to keep things moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these tasks yourself, or is it okay with you if someone else does them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d rather handle it myself, yes. Not because folks can’t do anything, but they don’t keep up. The move, the garage, church things, even the younger guys at work… it just feels like I’m the one with enough push to get it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're thinking about these tasks over and over, even when you're trying to do something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I suppose I think about what needs doing, but I wouldn’t say it’s over and over like that. Just things that have to get handled.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for not doing things perfectly?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I like things done right, but I don’t sit around beating myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to perform tasks in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Specific way? Not really, no. I just want things done decent, you know… but I don’t have some set way it has to be every time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble relaxing or unwinding when you're not working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t sit still too well. I’ll be in a chair a few minutes and then I’m up messing with boxes, measuring something out in the garage, loading tools… or I end up going back over to the old place. Just feels like my body’s got a motor in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, I don’t think. I get things on my mind, sure, but I wouldn’t say they repeat over and over where I can’t shake them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're not actively working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I get uneasy, I guess. If I’m just sitting there, it doesn’t last long. I’m up sorting boxes or measuring around the garage, loading tools… sometimes I just head back to the old place. Feels like I’ve got a motor in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things are not done properly unless you do them yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I just feel like if I don’t keep after it, things won’t get handled right. The move, the garage, stuff at church… even the younger guys at work. I’m not trying to be difficult, I just seem to be the one with the drive to make sure it’s done properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if things aren't done the way you want them to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that something bad would happen, exactly. I just… I don’t like leaving things sloppy or half-done. It bothers me, but I can’t say I’m expecting some disaster.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all these tasks to achieve a certain standard or to prevent something from going wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s more about getting things done right. Everybody keeps saying I’m doing too much, but… to me, the move won’t handle itself, the new garage needs to be built right, church things need organizing. And the younger fellows at work still need to be shown how the job ought to be done. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts are ever good enough?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “good enough” is a hard way to put it. I just see what still needs doing, and I keep at it. I guess I don’t spend much time deciding if it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that if you didn't do all these tasks, something would be missing or incomplete?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose it would feel incomplete. The move isn’t going to handle itself, and the garage needs to be done right. Same with things at church, and even the younger guys at work. I just feel like I’m the one with the drive to see it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you're doing all these tasks is more because you want everything to be perfect or because you're trying to avoid mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s either one exactly. I just… see what needs doing, and I do it. I don’t think of it as perfection so much. I just don’t like leaving things half-done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would stop doing all these tasks if someone else could do them just as well as you?\n\nPatient: If someone could really do it the same way… maybe I’d let some of it go. But I don’t see that happening much. The move, the garage, church things… even the younger fellows at work, I just seem to be the one with the drive to keep it moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can take a break and come back to a task later without worrying about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can step away if I have to, but it tends to sit there in my mind. I don’t much like leaving it hanging.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're driven more by wanting to achieve something perfectly or by wanting to avoid making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about perfect, exactly, or being afraid of mistakes. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done—the move, the garage, church stuff, even showing the younger guys at work how it ought to go. Everyone says it’s too much, but… I don’t see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious or worried if someone else did one of these tasks and didn't do it exactly as you would?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxious. I might notice it, sure… if it wasn’t how I’d do it. But I don’t know that I’d sit there worrying over it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I’m just trying to keep things moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these tasks yourself, or is it okay with you if someone else does them?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’d rather handle it myself, yes. Not because folks can’t do anything, but they don’t keep up. The move, the garage, church things, even the younger guys at work… it just feels like I’m the one with enough push to get it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're thinking about these tasks over and over, even when you're trying to do something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I suppose I think about what needs doing, but I wouldn’t say it’s over and over like that. Just things that have to get handled.\n\nDoctor: Do you often criticize yourself for not doing things perfectly?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I like things done right, but I don’t sit around beating myself up over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel a strong urge to perform tasks in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Specific way? Not really, no. I just want things done decent, you know… but I don’t have some set way it has to be every time.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble relaxing or unwinding when you're not working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. I don’t sit still too well. I’ll be in a chair a few minutes and then I’m up messing with boxes, measuring something out in the garage, loading tools… or I end up going back over to the old place. Just feels like my body’s got a motor in it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, I don’t think. I get things on my mind, sure, but I wouldn’t say they repeat over and over where I can’t shake them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're not actively working on something?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I get uneasy, I guess. If I’m just sitting there, it doesn’t last long. I’m up sorting boxes or measuring around the garage, loading tools… sometimes I just head back to the old place. Feels like I’ve got a motor in me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like things are not done properly unless you do them yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe. I just feel like if I don’t keep after it, things won’t get handled right. The move, the garage, stuff at church… even the younger guys at work. I’m not trying to be difficult, I just seem to be the one with the drive to make sure it’s done properly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if things aren't done the way you want them to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that something bad would happen, exactly. I just… I don’t like leaving things sloppy or half-done. It bothers me, but I can’t say I’m expecting some disaster.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all these tasks to achieve a certain standard or to prevent something from going wrong?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s more about getting things done right. Everybody keeps saying I’m doing too much, but… to me, the move won’t handle itself, the new garage needs to be built right, church things need organizing. And the younger fellows at work still need to be shown how the job ought to be done. I don’t know… I just seem to have the drive for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your efforts are ever good enough?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… “good enough” is a hard way to put it. I just see what still needs doing, and I keep at it. I guess I don’t spend much time deciding if it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that if you didn't do all these tasks, something would be missing or incomplete?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose it would feel incomplete. The move isn’t going to handle itself, and the garage needs to be done right. Same with things at church, and even the younger guys at work. I just feel like I’m the one with the drive to see it through.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the reason you're doing all these tasks is more because you want everything to be perfect or because you're trying to avoid mistakes?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s either one exactly. I just… see what needs doing, and I do it. I don’t think of it as perfection so much. I just don’t like leaving things half-done.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would stop doing all these tasks if someone else could do them just as well as you?\n\nPatient: If someone could really do it the same way… maybe I’d let some of it go. But I don’t see that happening much. The move, the garage, church things… even the younger fellows at work, I just seem to be the one with the drive to keep it moving.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can take a break and come back to a task later without worrying about it?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I can step away if I have to, but it tends to sit there in my mind. I don’t much like leaving it hanging.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're driven more by wanting to achieve something perfectly or by wanting to avoid making mistakes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s about perfect, exactly, or being afraid of mistakes. I just feel like things need doing, and I’m the one with enough drive to get them done—the move, the garage, church stuff, even showing the younger guys at work how it ought to go. Everyone says it’s too much, but… I don’t see it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious or worried if someone else did one of these tasks and didn't do it exactly as you would?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it anxious. I might notice it, sure… if it wasn’t how I’d do it. But I don’t know that I’d sit there worrying over it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be satisfied if things were done well, but not perfectly?\n\nPatient: I suppose so… if it’s done well enough. I don’t know that I need everything perfect. I just like it done right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, I guess. I put money down for the garage work and some equipment without really talking it over with my wife first. And I kept driving back and forth between the houses, rushing things at the plant… people told me I was taking chances, but I didn’t really stop to think it through."
